A Cause for Jubilation
Aldis • August 1, 1010 AE
Ch. 1 • To Survive a Moondevil
Ch. 2 • Skulls & Schemes
Ch. 3 • The Caravan
Ch. 4 • A Night In
Ch. 5 • The Currency of Coffee
Ch. 6 • The Blood Moon
Ch. 7 • Family
Woozy 12/1/2025 5:48 PM
Normally, the desert was supposed to get cold at night. Though the sun had set about an hour ago with only the faintest of pinks on the far horizon saying their final goodbyes, the air clung to the midday heat as though it were afraid to let go and let the chill of night take its place. The lights of Jubilation flickered similarly on the horizon, just distant enough to work as a sort of guiding light back to civilization for a lone figure sitting on a rock. Just far enough that the noise of civilization didn't carry out here, the evening trills of birds, rodents, and insects calling out their nighttime routines. The noise of the night was joined by the small spark of flint and metal igniting the contents of a pipe that dangled between the man's lips. A tail of smoke rose, curling away in the modest wind with the scent of tobacco. He exhaled another plume and settled back on his rock, tucking away the flint before resting the hand on a well-worn case beside him. It had straps fit for carrying on one's back, with polished metal latches and a lock to keep the contents secure inside. The lock had been unlatched in quiet anticipation, though the case remained closed. He fancied himself as a sort of travelling salesman at times. It wasn't his primary goal in any town, though he always seemed to have the right thing on hand for someone's ails. He liked keeping a diverse supply for a diverse collection of buyers, just in case. Earlier in the day he'd been schmoozing in town and had a potential buyer lined up for some kind of libidinous oils. He remembered he had something that suited that sort of need, and set up a nice private meeting place. To defend their dignity, of course. Just needed to wait for them to arrive now, but thankfully waiting was something he wasn't half bad at. Another plume of smoke rose, exhaled through his nose, and he looked skyward for a moment towards the beckoning moon.
Daarka 12/1/2025 6:44 PM
"Ain't wise. Sittin' all alone." The voice was a rasping hiss like shifting sands. Looking towards the sound, the man would find the burning yellow eyes of a gangly coyote standing a stone's throw away, that gaze boring a hole in him with unblinking intensity. It camouflaged well into the scenery, and its head hung low from its shoulders, ears pinned forward, unmoving. The animal was average in size for a coyote, if not a bit on the larger side--but scrawny. Disheveled, its fur matted in some places with neglect. The man wouldn't have to be any expert in the native fauna to identify something was deeply wrong with the animal, whether by way of affliction or famine--or some combination of the two. This was further emphasized by the fathomless hunger in that unwavering gaze... and the dark clumps of fur where something had splattered and dried to the color of rust. For a beat, the man could be forgiven for wondering if he had hallucinated the voice. But when it spoke up again, he'd have no trouble sourcing it from the animal's moving mouth, following the quick swipe of the tongue over its muzzle. "You lost, old man?" (edited)
Woozy 12/1/2025 7:08 PM
The voice had the old man's head immediately on a swivel, orange-brown eyes fixating on those bright unblinking yellow ones. His gaze drifted briefly around the creature for anyone - or anything - else, then back to the matted, scruffy fur. It wasn't unusual to see an animal with dried blood. It was for one to speak, though, and he had a feeling it wasn't another beast that it'd spilled it from. Though his nerves were up and alert, he couldn't deny the immediate curiosity such a dark revelation had. There was a small squint to his eyes as he smiled, the whites of his teeth just visible. "As a matter of fact, I do happen to be sitting all the way out here with a purpose." He paused to take another puff from his pipe, letting the smoke curl about as lazily as he sat. He shifted forward, placing an elbow on one skinny leg to hold his pipe off to the side. A slight nod of his head went towards the case. "I'm waitin on a fellow lookin to purchase a few goods of a... sensitive nature, as it were. I can't imagine you're here to collect on his behalf, no. I imagine you're after somethin else. What does a coyote go lookin for when no one else's lookin?"
Daarka 12/1/2025 7:31 PM
There was a silence that stretched. Another swipe of the tongue. And then a slow, measured step forward--which drew attention to how unusually long the animal's toes were. Less paws than they were fine manipulators, though they bore the trappings of coyote anatomy otherwise, including the thick, slightly overgrown claws they ended with. "Goes lookin' for lost old men," that voice rasped in reply, something about the cadence almost sounding intoxicated. The words were followed by another slow step forward, the movement and posture framing the lone man as little more than an imperiled hare being stalked. But the visual was wrong. It was slow to register, but by the time the man would recognize the uncanny valley, he was already deep in it. It wasn't just the coyote's digits that were unusual; it was also distinctly unnatural for the animal's form to rise in height as it slithered closer, the motions fluid but illogical as shoulder blades peaked up higher than they had been before, matted fur shifting and--sloughing, almost, like dirty tangles dripping free in dreadlocks that fell to the sand to make way for the hide that stretched to new dimensions. It was gradual, but also seemed to happen in the blink of an eye--and the man would find himself staring at something closer in size to a horse than a coyote, limbs elongated as if halfheartedly trying to take a human shape without caring to get the rest of the anatomy right. "Your client ain't comin'," that voice came again, now flanged with a low echo in on itself. "Got hung up down the way. I can take you to 'em."
Woozy 12/1/2025 7:56 PM
It was a good thing his pipe had already come to rest by his side. He'd have nearly dropped it by now if he hadn't already lowered it, watching the way the coyote in front of him shifted in ways animals ain't meant to move, grew in ways that went against the tides of normal progression. Despite the warmth in the air, the presence ahead of him earned a cold trill down his back. He'd been slouched before, but now he sat straight up, legs shifting to pull him to a stand off to the side of the rock so that it wasn't behind him. Though he did not run the motion naturally put just an extra smidge of distance between the two, for what little good it did as the creature stalked closer. Understanding seemed to dawn on his face, snippets of conversation in town aligning like gears in a freshly repaired watch ticking together in perfect unison as he swiftly understood just what he was looking at. And something about that revelation steeled his spine. One could be forgiven if the darkness in his shadows seemed to ripple ever so slightly and grow in the desert heat, like the eye from a stranger in a nearby booth that'd only been half listening fully turned on the pair. One could be forgiven if that shadow seemed to crawl up from behind the man, seemed to manifest as a hand on one of those white-robed shoulders from the shadows of his dreadlocks, and grip. The man shivered again, eyes closing for a moment before fixing again on the coyote in front of him. There had been brown in the eyes before, but now that orange was unmistakable and dominating. There was a click of his tongue and a wide grin, the man leaning forward. When he spoke again, it was with almost a hissing laugh behind the voice. "You must one of them 'moondevils' everyone's been talking about, ain't ya? What a surprise, a genuine delight even. Frankly, you can keep whatever you did with the rest of that man to yourself, we've no need of him if he ain't got any coin on him to spend. This one though, this one is Mine, understand?"
Daarka 12/1/2025 8:06 PM
The monster stilled its forward momentum. A beat later, the jaw that had fallen a little slack slowly closed. That silence stretched a tense moment more, the creature's expression unreadable but unblinking. Then, gradually, almost dazedly, the moondevil slowly slumped back to sit on its haunches, its nose twitching minutely with exploratory little sniffs. "... Fuck are you," it replied. The lilting, predatory hiss it had spoken in before had flattened, and what couldn't be gleaned from facial expression was evident in the fully derailed confusion of that tone.
Woozy 12/1/2025 9:04 PM
Both man and voice laughed, and the figure moved to settle back onto his rock in perfect comfort as the coyote slumped down. "Kin, in a sense. Certainly not one of you, no. More of a... distant cousin." The shadow rippled again, more clearly this time, like a pair of shoulders shrugging off a coat until it more properly pulled free of the man's silhouette into its own. Though the body resembled a lagomorph about as much as Echo's resembled a coyote, there were a few additional differences in the length of aquatic tail replacing the short cottontail approach of regular hares. Long grippy fingers in a similar fashion to the coyote's, and aquatic fins adorning the head in the shape of ears. If it appeared to be too much like prey at first glance though, the sharp carnivorous teeth behind the distinctive incisors. The shadow extended a long arm out towards the coyote as though for a shake. "You can call me Fable, of the Luck clan. That shouldn't mean anything to you though, so don't worry yourself none if it doesn't." The man, free of the overbearing nature of his shadow, flashed the coyote a similar smile and settled back with his pipe, nodding with it as though tipping a hat. "Hazzel, nice to meetcha. What's your name then?"
Daarka 12/1/2025 9:17 PM
That vague sense of inebriation was still present indistinctly, manifesting now in how long it was taking the moondevil to parse this turn of events. When it seemed to hit some cognitive wall, it shook out its scruff, as if in an attempt to clear its head. The tongue over its muzzle now was less a matter of salivating in anticipation, more focused on sweeping over its nose in a furthered effort towards clarity. Its eyes were still the same glinting yellow as before, but now squinting slightly as it shifted between the mortal(?) man and the far more comprehensible--to them, anyways--shade. Fortunately, for all parties involved, that appearance of that entity seemed well and truly divorced from the things that whet the moondevil's appetite. When it replied after another stilted pause, it was with a rarity: honesty. "Ain't got one," that voice echoed plainly. "Never needed one." Another sweep of the tongue over its nose, before it went back to twitching with that creeping curiosity, even if the beast still seemed deeply bewildered. "What you need a mortal for."
Woozy 12/1/2025 9:34 PM
"No name, hm? That's a shame." Hazzel said, taking another puff of his pipe. When a handshake seemed to not be on the way, he silently offered the pipe the shade's way, who took it for a puff of his own. "Not a surprise, though." The shade replied in casual observation. There was no pity there, only understanding. A Name could be dangerous to have, depending on where or who you were. The pair seemed content to let the coyote take its time, and with the threat deescalated the man's gaze drifted skyward again towards the moon. The shade's gaze remained on their new companion though, and he let out a laugh at the question. "Mortals can get around with a lot less fuss here than we can on our own. A lot less eyes on me if he's the one doing the walking, you feel? Means I don't need a disguise to maintain for it either... and I'm sure you understand the appeal of havin some company along for the ride." He let that hang in the air, sniffing the air before frowning. "... Where's the rest of you?"
Daarka 12/1/2025 9:42 PM
One ear twitched slightly at that. Some twinge of emotion the creature wasn't expecting to have stirred--though not one it seemed to be angered by. "You're lookin' at 'em," they replied grimly after a moment, head twisting to run its tongue over the fur on its chest, beginning what would require far more work of getting the dried blood out. Still, that intensity ebbing from the way their attention had been transfixed on the stranger was indication enough that they were no longer in the crosshairs for the time being, that some unspoken recategorization had occurred, even if it had simultaneously made the moondevil little more than a tortoise flipped onto its back as it transparently tried to remember what interactions entailed when active predation was removed from them. Almost as soon as it started on the halfhearted attempt to groom, it abandoned the venture, head swiveling back to the other demon. "You must be new here."
Woozy 12/1/2025 10:06 PM
Fable let out a disquieted grunt at their answer. There was a measure of understanding in his gaze that suggested he knew what that implied, providing context to their earlier demeanor even beyond the normal stalking of a mortal man for the sin of being alone in the wilderness. "New enough, but not too new to hear from a few townsfolk crowing about the good work they've been puttin in every night out here. I just wasn't quite inclined to take their word on how many they'd missed, just in case they were exaggerating." Hazzel's attention slipped back to the moondevil from the moon. "Few of em were fixing up for another sweep while the full moon's out tonight. If you've got a den around here, you'll probably not want to go back til after the sun's come up."
Daarka 12/1/2025 10:17 PM
The creature heaved a long, thin sigh. At the mention of the full moon, their own attention lifted, glancing up at the celestial body that reflected back almost exaggeratedly in its eyes. Its ears flattened slightly when it dropped its head back down to reply. "... Don't remember where a den is," they grunted lowly, the admission carrying the least of the lilting enthusiasm they had initially approached with. If coyotes could develop bags under their eyes, it seemed likely they would have severe ones. But it contextualized the vague air of inebriation better, the mildly labored processing behind every word spoken. At least, every word spoken earnestly--but even the initial, more threatening ones had been a bit cognitively shallow. These creatures were said to be quite a bit more cunning than to just directly approach prey with ominous taunts. Granted, Hazzel would have been run down without issue if left uninterrupted, but the brutish approach was a desperate one. "If they find me... reckon I'll get a good meal in," they rasped, though the words sounded more wryly spiteful than they did confident.
Woozy 12/1/2025 11:32 PM
The pair shared a glance at the confession, some unspoken dialogue working between them briefly. Wordlessly the shade passed the pipe to Hazzel, who took another puff before handing it back and slid off the rock onto his feet. The shade stayed where he sat even as Hazzel picked up his cane and pulled out a large tome which hovered over his empty hand lightly. In the short distance behind them where a decent patch of clearing lay, he began dragging his peg leg in the ground, murmuring the incantations of a spell - quietly, so as not to interrupt or detract from the evening's ambiance. The shade took another puff from the pipe, smoke filtering out from six openings down his neck, arranged in a way that could suggest gills. "More like when they find you at this rate. You won't take out enough of em to make it worth it in your current state, not right now. Good night of rest or two in you, now that'd be a different story." He glanced over his shoulder towards Hazzel's work in a brief redirection of attention. "As it would happen, we'll be setting up camp out here for the night. There'll be a nice spot inside for you to get some proper sleep, and none of them righteous bastards from town'll be able to do a lick of anything to ya while you're inside." He looked back towards them, then pointedly over their shoulder. "Anything left of that man from earlier? I can have him clean up the mess just in case if there is... help throw off the scent."
Daarka 12/1/2025 11:41 PM
Another labored moment to process. A slight squint of confusion as they grappled what they least expected to ever encounter: charity. Then a glint of some tiny light back into the yellow eyes as something distantly clicked. Being reminded of the meal had that tongue flash over its maw another time. "Blood in the sand. Clothes." In the silence that followed, it seemed that if there were more on that list, it had evaded their memory. "You know 'em?" Despite the question, there wasn't a lick of remorse in the words.
Woozy 12/2/2025 12:01 AM
"I think his name was Charles," Fable drawled. "Decent man, as most folks out here like to think they are. Someone'll miss him, probably, but not his wife. She might even be inclined to thank you for your services if she didn't think she were a more decent woman than she is. But no, I don't know 'im. Not enough to care. There's a dozen more men just like him back in Jubilation, and a dozen more towns just like Jubilation." He paused to think. " He didn't happen to have any money on him, did he? If he was coming out here for some of our tonics, I'd hope he brought something worth spending."
Daarka 12/2/2025 12:17 AM
Another long pause. But this one seemed less an issue of foggy cognition and more contemplative. The moondevil's head cocked to turn an eye towards where Hazzel was carving a rune circle in the dirt, and they just... watched him for a moment. Eventually, they seemed to find some manner of concluding in whatever their mess of thoughts were, stirring again--this time, to rise to their feet once more. To only two of their feet, in fact, their front end rearing back as if they were a very thin, stretched-out bear. Logically, it should have made them stand incredibly tall, given how they were about eye level with Hazzel in this form even on all fours. But by the time they stood fully upright, their eye level was only just a head or so above that point it had been at prior, as another confusing arrangement of malleable proportions and sloughing mats of fur fell away like a loose layer, until the figure was shockingly indistinguishable from a human. The eyes were the same, sharp and yellow in a way humans didn't exactly have them. The tawny, sun-speckled complexion and dirty-blonde hair in its unkempt tangles of collar-length strands was certainly a bleed-through of the demon's natural form's coloration, too. But anyone who hadn't seen them a moment prior would be hard-pressed to find cause for suspicion. This form, though, did very much have bags under their eyes, despite the sharpness in that gaze. Their lips were a little chapped, their clothes in a similar state of neglect as their fur had been--and the enshrouding, ruddy-hued poncho concealing what was likely a similarly emaciated build beneath. There was a lazy slouch to their posture, though it was unclear how much of that was their normal posture versus delirious weakness. A hand snaked under that poncho, and a moment later reemerged holding a linen drawstring pouch in their spidery fingers that mutedly jingled as it moved with the tell-tale sound of coin. It didn't look stuffed to the gills--but it didn't look pathetically empty, either. They weighed it in their hand a moment more, eyeing the shade contemplatively. But didn't linger in that pause too long, before swinging an underhand toss to send the coins their way. "Fair's fair," they rasped, the unnatural echo gone from their voice--though the dryness of it remained. (edited)
Woozy 12/2/2025 1:15 PM
As their attention shifted to Hazzel so did the shades, though it was more in a companionitive sort of joint watching as opposed to any proper oogling. He knew what this spell was and what it looked like to cast it already, after all. Though he was the focus of both pairs of eyes, Hazzel continued with his ritual spell as though neither of them were there, evidently comfortable enough to turn his back to the moondevil. An unwise decision for anyone else, though the situation had clearly moved on from such cautions for the time being. Fable’s attention returned to the movement in front of him, unsurprised by the shifting into a more humanoid shape. His expression remained mildly amused with a level of firm patience underneath it, letting them take all the time they needed. He himself stayed exactly where he was, as he was. A shadow didn’t need to hide in the night. Despite his lazy posture echoing Hazzel’s from earlier, the hand that shot out to intercept the bag of coins was quick and accurate. He raised the back briefly to test its heft and feel, nodding to himself after a moment. “Fair’s fair,” He echoed back. Behind the pair almost in perfect synchronicity, Hazzel’s spell tingled briefly in the air before his Tiny Hut apparated into its designated spot. It looked like a decent little tent-hut from the outside, a string of wooden bead curtain blocking visibility inside. The man let out a satisfied hmm at its arrival, shutting his book with a heavy sound. “Hut’s up,” He announced, turning to walk back over to the pair. As he walked his book returned to its place at his hip, and the now-empty hand gesturing out another quick spell, the verbal component a quick whisper under his lips until another man seemed to just as quickly walk forward where he’d been with little more than the ripple of shadows around him telling someone else was there. This new man looked a bit taller than Hazzel was, a bit more portly with pale skin and black hair with the trappings of a common Talmas man. He twirled his simple cane a moment before leaning against it, giving the moondevil a polite smile. “Feel free to make yourself comfortable inside. I’ll go clean up that mess so it don’t attract any attention we ain’t lookin for. You just leave Jubilation to us for the night.” He said before continuing his walk into the desert, silhouette set up against the distant lights of town. At the invitation, Fable finally rose from his seat on the rock and began making his way towards the hut, a head gesture towards it in further invitation to the not-coyote. Now that he was moving, it was easy to see the shade was missing the same foot the old man was. “Think there’s a mancala set inside, if you’re in the mood for a game.” He called back.
Daarka 12/2/2025 5:27 PM
Gaze now lidded, the moondevil watched the conjured figure join Hazzel in fucking off to go scrub blood out of the sand, before their attention was dragged back to Fable. With a little less hesitation than they'd been operating with thus far, they soon sauntered after him, their stride just as slouched and lazy as their posture. Ducking through the beaded curtain, their eyes swept over the space, still seeming a little dazed. Did they know how to play mancala? They couldn't remember. So... no, functionally. "Haven't played," they rasped, the two-word lie a little kinder on their dignity than continuing to express just how rotten their brain had become. Not that it mattered much when that reality wasn't hard to deduce otherwise. "Always drawn more to cards, anyways." They oozed down to sit on one of the cots, gaze still taking its time traveling around the little details of the space. Conversation. How did civil conversation go? They were good at it. They were supposed to be good at it. They hadn't cared to try to remember in weeks, and now found themselves pushing back against a fog with the consistency of molasses. "What you here for. Somethin' here for you?"
Woozy 12/2/2025 7:38 PM
If the shade picked up on his lie he was gracious enough to ignore it, letting out an intrigued hum that sounded suspiciously like someone about to introduce them to one of their favorite games. He slouched on the other cot, tail curling around where that missing foot was, and reached over to a small woven basket. The lid flicked away carelessly, and a hand briefly dipped into the shadows to draw out a deck of cards. "Talmas Hold-em then." His hands moved expertly into a shuffle in the space between them, whistling an unrecognizable tune that seemed to bow more to whimsy than sheet music. He glanced up from the cards at the pair of questions, hands pausing briefly before doling out their hands. "Nothing's here for me except me. Wasn't too keen on sticking around back home with nothin to do and constant peacocking with the other clans. Let em sweat it out a little with me gone." He swept up his hand of cards, drawing them close before digging into that coin purse and tossing down a pair of coins in the middle. A wager. If the moondevil expected to be asked to contribute though, he said nothing of it and instead gestured for them to begin. "Your lot from around here or didja migrate from somewhere else?"
Daarka 12/2/2025 9:05 PM
The moondevil sank off the cot to sit on the floor, slouching like an ooze despite how illogical that seemed for their bony anatomy. Somewhere in the process, though there was no clarity as to when, two gold coins were produced--and muscle memory pierced through the mind fog enough for them to be deftly rolled off the back of their knuckles and neatly flicked to join the ones Fable had put down. Those long fingers unhurriedly extended to sweep the hand dealt towards themselves, lifting it and spreading it to look over with the slow, deliberate touch of someone grazing fingers over a fragile relic. "Born of the desert," they rattled distractedly. "Always been of the desert. If there was ancestry otherwise, wasn't worth passin' down. Will die in the desert. Will kill a lot more in the desert first." They had been actually assessing their hand of cards at first, but their gaze stared through the cards now. "They like killin' under the moon. Gonna watch 'em die under it."
Woozy 12/2/2025 11:49 PM
Fable let a hum rumble through his chest at their answer. The hoot of a desert owl earned a brief pause, the call of natural wildlife going about its business a pleasant and brief immersion in the desert around them. Though he'd gotten used to the dust and the dry air, it had never been home to him the way it was to them. "Must be nice, having a cradle like that for your whole life. You could certainly pick worse places to eke out a life in. The Arctic.. much too cold. Cities are fine, lots of opportunity, but too many rules. Here though... It suits. This place was made for you as much as you were made for it." He waited for them to start their hand, though their turn seemed to never come as their thoughts went distant. After a few long moments later he took another puff from the pipe. "I hear it'll be a blood moon soon. Gets em squirrely. Anxious. Can't think of a better night to set a trap for em... Hazzel can spread a rumor pretty easy about seein the last moondevil, right before it. Bet we can find a good ol cove to lead em into." He paused, thoughtful. "There's a benefit to hiding the remains in some remote cave or hole, keeps it harder to interrupt, but disposing of it in town square for everyone to see in the morning... I reckon that'd rattle any survivors."
Daarka 12/3/2025 12:19 AM
That was certainly sufficient to draw the tawny demon’s gaze up, pinning it on the shadowy entity opposite it. The yellow stare was hard to read for a long pause, thoughts cluttering behind their eyes and gradually congealing into tangible shapes. Then sharp, pointy shapes. Like the ones that they bared as a fanged, lopsided grin split their face. It wasn’t a kind expression. “Reckon I like the way you think.” The words were quiet, nearly hoarse. But heavy. Distinctly conclusive, like a big, black period nailed to the end of an unfinished and equally dark thought. That settled that. Settled something. Their attention lazily slithered back to the cards in hand, and they unhurriedly moved to take their turn. “… Don’t know if I’ll ever be full again,” they went on idly. “Ain’t never been so ravenous.” A beat. “… Maybe I was born this hungry.” Couldn’t remember that, either. “Ain’t about the food, though. Not really. Got hares and javelina for that. They die too pretty. They ain’t got sins to asphyxiate on. Their blood tastes good and natural. What you supposed to eat is hares and javelina. So they die good and sweet ‘cause they suppose to. It’s what they here for. Men deserve to die. Men go killin’ shit they ain’t put here to kill. Guttin’ them puts right what they set wrong. They die delicious. Don’t know I’ll ever be full of it.” The words had remained quiet. Still ever so faintly slurred. A walking red flag, perhaps, to any conversational partner who was not themselves a demon. But they seemed to have latched on to some feeble thread of cognitive function, at least, the card game between them simultaneously only occupying half their focus, and also tethering them to the tangible world of sanity through its ritualistic motions.
Woozy 12/3/2025 12:55 PM
The grin on the shadow’s face grew to nearly match theirs, enjoying the reaction from his suggestions. Something about the way this card game, this conversation, seemed to slowly wind back the whole ball of yarn that’d become the moondevil’s mind into something tangible seemed satisfying, like navigating a puzzle box full of knives hiding a stick of dynamite for a single cookie. “I reckoned you would.” His gaze drifted back to the cards as they took their turn, though the raise in his ears confirmed he was still intent on listening, and he nodded along conversationally. Licked his lips a moment as the conversation turned towards bellies and fullness. The shadows did well to hide most of his expressions unless he wanted them seen, but there was no hiding the internal thoughts rising at the topic from his eyes. A trick of the light, perhaps. He drew a fresh card for his hand, then pulled a pair free to settle down in front of him. “Maybe. Maybe you were born this way, born to hunger and gnash for the sweet sins of men. I can certainly relate… Once you taste the finer things in life, something real nice, everything else just pales in comparison.” He paused, squinting past the cards for a moment. A quiet contemplation before a mental shrug accompanying an inhale. “We could have a feast of all them townie fuckers. Think I saw a few winks of a nice twisty canyon not too far from here that’d trap em well. Think I heard the song of something hollow underneath, too. Might have a nice cavern down below. Could get creative with that. What you think?”
Daarka 12/3/2025 1:29 PM
They didn't respond immediately, the echoes of the grin still lingering absentmindedly on their face as their gaze settled back to the cards. For a while, as their hands moved slow but steady to take their own turn, it seemed they were just focusing on the game. "Think I'm still confused about the man," they ultimately rasped in another stroke of honesty. The admission didn't sound loaded or judgmental, necessarily--but earnestly baffled. "Holds his own mind, don't he? Ain't quite as hollow as a puppet. Awfully cheerful to bend to your whim, though." Their tongue ran slowly over their teeth a moment. "Ain't concerned much 'bout what makes you share my interests. What's he gettin' out of it? Or do he ain't got a choice."
Woozy 12/4/2025 9:19 PM
A low chuckle escaped the shade at the earnestness of the question. Not that it wasn’t a fair one to make. They were right in that it made sense the pair of demons would find some decent common ground, but the man was, well. Most men liked to think they were too good to do things like openly cavort with demons, unless they thought they had a damned good reason to do it. “Astute observation. No, he ain’t a puppet. Does a lot of the steering day to day even, when I’m feelin lazy.” He said, pulling another card. "He sticks with me because at the end of the day, he has a lot more in common with you'n I than he does with the rest of humanity. Sure, he's got some goodly aspirations. Help the poor, feed the needy, rescuin kittens. That don't stop him from being willing to burn it all down if the situation calls for it. I've helped him do it before." Woozy 12/5/2025 7:55 PM

Woozy 12/6/2025 11:47 PM
The bitterness certainly was understandable, and the man was willing to listen quietly and play his cards with an ear fully given to the coyote's teeth on the matter. Daarka 12/14/2025 1:32 AM
The morning service at the chapel of Jubilation was a busy affair, with most of the town cramming into the generously large building in their Sunday best, fanning away the warm desert morning air and dutifully listening to one very, very pious man project dogma from the diaphragm. Service stretched an excessive two hours. Plenty of time for one well-dressed young lady to listen in for the first hour, before discretely but abruptly excusing herself as if suddenly gripped with a private emergency. The purpose in her steps and light look of queasy concern on her face was enough to thwart the judgmental stares of the people she quietly passed on her way out, back into the relatively empty streets of the town. Fanning herself lightly with one hand to relieve whatever fever had taken hold of her, those purposeful steps carried her down the street, until the few straggling passerby who were not themselves attending the sermon in the chapel had lost sight of her. The fanning stopped then. The forward momentum did not. The priest had a house not far from the chapel. It wasn’t hard to find now that the woman knew his scent; he’d walked directly there from that home. He seemed to keep goodly neighbors. The kind who were all in that chapel with him presently. But dogs didn’t go to church. As the woman’s pace had slowed to a more leisurely stroll, she found the source of that scent, too; a pretty white hound chained to the shade of the porch. It lifted its head at her approach, but something kept it from exercising its duties as guardian of the property. Maybe it was the knowing look in the woman’s eyes, or the way she lifted her finger wordlessly over her lips towards the beast. On the porch of the priest’s home on that empty street, she slowly approached the dog and leaned to reach towards it. Ears splayed, tail tucked, it curled away from her in nervous submission, but she ultimately got fingers around its collar—and slid it off over its head. Dropped it there on the porch, leaving the dog loose where it cowered. She turned and left then. Somewhere along the way, between the buildings empty of the pious, the fancy dress and womanly flesh withered away, crumbling, shriveling, sloughing and warping until all that was left was a pretty white hound without a collar. Daarka 12/14/2025 8:54 PM

Daarka 12/14/2025 11:44 PM

Daarka 12/15/2025 10:17 PM

Woozy 12/16/2025 1:17 PM
Hazzel wasn’t hard to find. Though Jubilation was a pious town, he’d spent a good evening carousing politely at the bar, extracting coppers and whispers and gossip from the townsfolk. As luck would have it, he’d finally pulled himself away from the quiet reverie to step outside for some fresh air. Woozy 12/17/2025 5:24 AM
Hazzel leaned in to listen, weight shifted onto his cane to do it. The remnants of his pipe dumped to the ground and were consequently scuffed out by the toe of his boot, replaced by a fine powder from the bag This he lit again, and though he tucked it between the corner of his lips once more he distinctly let it burn on its own, filling the alley with the heady aroma of incense. “Heads or tails?” He asked, pulling out a coin from his purse to rub under his thumb against the crook of his pointer finger. The flick of his gaze towards the shadows instead of the moondevil in front of him suggested it wasn’t them he was asking. After a moment, he huffed in quiet amusement. “You always pick tails.” Satisfied with that, he flipped the coin in the air, expertly slapping it down onto the back of his hand. “Week from now, north side of Talmas.” He declared before peeling away his hand, revealing the back side of the coin glinting in the night. Tails. “Northwest side.” Another slap to the hand, then revealed. Heads. He frowned, though it was more of a thinking expression, like he was playing a game of memory. “Cadence side?” He asked, flipping a third time. A final slap, and heads revealed once more. He stared at the coin for a few moments, chewing on the answer. “So,” He drawled slowly. “If she’s keepin her usual route - and I’ve never heard her do otherwise - 7 days she should be abouts near Amnesty.” Campfire orange eyes flicked back up towards yellow. “That work for you?”
Daarka 12/23/2025 7:38 PM
The moondevil watched with acute interest, but without interrupting the ritual in its entirety. They were too wise to some of the variables in play to mistake the coin flips for anything other than precisely what they were. As Hazzel drew his final conclusion, their eyes lifted from the coin back to his. “Near Amnesty in seven days. I can work with that,” they rasped. “Could get there and back within the same twenty-four hours. With any luck,” they added wryly, “ain’t even long enough for anyone to recognize me gone. How fast can you hop with that hare’s foot you holdin’ onto?”
Woozy 12/23/2025 8:29 PM
"I can hop pretty far, but for that.. Mm. I got a horse for that. Could do that, easy. You might have the benefit of being able to go on all fours, but no one's got time for me to hobble my way over there on one and a half." Hazzel grinned, wheezing out a little laugh. The ritual over, he tucked his coin back away and went back to leaning on his cane with one hand and holding his pipe with the other, letting it burn at its own pace. It'd be hard to get rid of the scent of it by the end of it - a scent that certainly brought back memories of the Hut. At least it was pleasant. It'd be a different story if it smelled like rotting carcasses.
Daarka 12/23/2025 8:43 PM

Woozy 12/23/2025 9:17 PM
"Six days," Hazzel agreed, shifting to lean against one of the dusty walls and roll his shoulders in anticipation. Six days was a lot of time. It was hardly any time at all, too. He'd have to find something to do to keep himself busy long enough for that, and then some. After all, needed to look like he was getting himself invested. He smacked his lips idly. Daarka 12/23/2025 9:55 PM
They looked back to Hazzel as the man offered his wisdom, eyes lidded slightly with the abstract heaviness hanging over them that they hadn’t exactly stopped to process. There had been no grieving period. But also not quite a need for one; they were not a man. Their family were not men. They did not die like men and they were not missed like men. They were not gone like men. But they were gone in their own way, and the violence and chaos of that uprooting—and the descent into near-complete madness that had followed—had robbed the lone survivor of any chance to adjust. Any chance to reorient themself. Remember which way was up. Redefine their new cadence of normality. But they decided it would be easier to make that adjustment when what remained of their kin was back with them again. And there was no mourning a demon like them did that was not a thing of vengeance and spite. Grander thoughts of such things were interrupted by the realization that Hazzel might be assuming the moondevil on the younger side. That curiosity captured their interest a moment, before they almost as abruptly decided there was nothing appealing about the truth there. “Ain’t the first time I’ve had to adapt,” they rumbled dryly. “Just the first time like this.” It wasn’t really a rejection of Hazzel’s sentiments, though. The bitterness coating their tongue was just a stubborn taste. “… You need anything from me in the meantime?” They went on, a long finger idly tapping where it hung against their thigh. “It’s time enough for petty crimes I can fan to good flames when I’m back. But nothin’ that has to be done ‘fore I can go, so nothin’ I can’t hold off on if there’s more use in me elsewhere.”
Woozy 12/23/2025 10:40 PM

Daarka 12/24/2025 12:28 AM
That last offer seemed at least a little unexpected, given the ever so subtle look of pause that flashed across their face. But one they accepted a moment later, giving a small dip of their chin as some nondescript part of them softened a fraction at the gesture. With nothing further to say and no inclination towards canned pleasantries (at least, not in authentic interactions such as these), they turned and slunk back down the alley, soon disappearing around a corner. Hazzel could be forgiven for feeling like their form had started to look strange at the last moment before he lost sight of them. Their evening was spent almost uneventfully, as they prowled through residential roads between family homes on that north side Hazzel had mentioned. At this hour, folks were in those homes. This wasn’t the hour for violations and trespass; this was an hour of observation, which was made easier with a very literal bird’s-eye view as a solitary crow casually flew from one yard to another, finding a place to perch in each and stay a moment to watch. There was scorn in that crow’s thoughts all the while. Idle at first, but growing more acute with each house they found lacking. Much of the buildings were constructed of plain wood, but most had at least some features painted, or the wood varnished in places. One yard bore a tree with a few low branches hacked off where they would have pressed against the building. One porch hung dusty wind chimes near a wedge that was perfectly suited for the nest of a bird, but vacant of one. One garden was home to a rose bush, unfamiliar with the brutality of the sun it lived under. For one small farm, the sole cow’s pen was latched with a mere loop of rope around the gate posts. Little rules broken. Little violations of the land that came first. Little defiances of forces that ought to have been honored. Little invitations to ill fortunes. It took the crow far longer than they were pleased with to find a single residence they deemed correct. Their eyes were keen enough to catch the glint of starlight off the sun-shrunk water in a wide but shallow clay pot that sat off the back porch; water, perhaps, for the dog the crow could smell but whom was presumably inside at present. But water for the dog was water for other passersby of similar stature. Or perching birds. Or swooping bats. Along the side of the house, no one had tamed the brittle bush that savored the shade cast by the wall it leaned against. To have hacked away at the wild plant would have torn up the good luck its roots spread beneath the foundation. This would do. The moondevil had no idea who lived here. It could have even been one of the hunters. Maybe even one who had put a bullet in the heads of their pack. But that wasn’t how rules worked, wasn’t something taken into consideration in this instance. Of all the homes, this one suited Luck best. What kind of luck would depend entirely on how clever the homeowner was. The moondevil did join Hazzel in the hut that night, and seemed to savor the company far more now that they were thinking more clearly. They had no trouble feeding themselves come dawn, the crepuscular hours drawing them out into the desert to hunt, before ultimately making their way back into Jubilation for the day. Over the next two days, they did little more than observe. It didn’t take long to single out the more affluent houses by the degree of finery the residents dressed themselves in. It was a more dedicated task to find patterns in routines, hours of occupation, times of rest. Noting the arrangements of each property; the doors that did not fully touch the threshold beneath them, the crawl spaces under foundations that looked up to rotting floorboards, the cracks in the wall panels where ants marched through to the kitchen, a bedroom window the child inside failed to latch, keyholes wide enough for a single jumping spider to pass through.
12:29 AM
On the third day, every coin that could be found within each residence went missing. The same day, (almost) the same sum of coins appeared under the master bed inside the house guarded by a brittle bush. On the fourth day, the moondevil had no plans but to laze around town. News of the robberies spread like wildfire, a thrum of alarm and confusion and unsettled paranoia saturating the day’s gossip. No busted locks or broken glass, no suspicious figures or any witnesses to anything at all. And no mention of the brittle bush home. Either a fortune not yet known, or a fortune maintained as good thus far. That night, the moondevil returned to the hut as they had been every night prior, and brought their own gossip back to Hazzel: the accurate kind. Because they needed someone to make a bet with; how long would it take—if at all—for the brittle bush’s good luck to sour? The coyote’s money was on two weeks’ time. Enough time for the brittle bush home to find the coin if they weren’t particularly observant—though depending on how oblivious they were, it could certainly stay hidden indefinitely—and then to fail to keep their secret. Perhaps they were a Talman of good morals who came forward to admit to the find in honesty. But there was no way to accidentally acquire the full sum of wealth of your neighbors. They would know this, and be faced with it’s guilt-feigning absurdity if honesty tugged at their heart. Perhaps they would try to silently pocket the coin and not draw attention to themselves. How long with that tell-tale heart beating beneath their floorboards before others found cause for suspicion? If the homeowner was married, was his wife prone to gossip with a friend she swore to secrecy? Surely that friend would only tell those she had sworn to secrecy, and them accordingly. Two weeks. The fifth day, as the gossip raged on—for all except them and Hazzel, it remained unanswered, of course—the moondevil found themselves drawn back to simpler roots. Another day of simple observation, followed by a night wherein they were much later to come back to the hut—but a goat, a barn cat, and a dog less hungry (and more bloody). They were much less chatty that night, settling in to groom away the gore before passing out contentedly, the food coma even sufficient to have them skip breakfast the next morning. So when that sixth morning came, the coyote was still present on the cot it had crashed on, mostly cleaned of the prior night’s meals (though not entirely) and slow to rise. Occasionally they stirred to lazily twist and roll on the cot, rubbing upside down against the covers to scratch along their back, before flopping limp again to continue to sleep in until Hazzel got up. Or the hut disappeared; whichever happened first. (edited)

Daarka 1/1/2026 5:14 PM
They shook their head at the offered water. "You need that more'n I do." Flopping back onto their haunches, they rested a moment while their panting evened out and gradually slowed. Just a minute or so, their head turned to scan the environment as they sat--before finding their second... eighth wind. The motion of rising back to their feet was complicated in a confusing slough of pelt, buckling inward of form, rising of trajectory, and finally a burst of black feathers as a crow took their place, fluttering up towards higher altitudes. Hazzel would be able to watch them for a time, until they were little more than a speck in the darkening sky that ultimately found a wind current to ride along as it watched the world below them. They would be gone like that for maybe ten, fifteen minutes... before the call of a crow reached back to Hazzel's position from a clear direction. Didn't seem like they could be fucked to fly all the way back to him. Following the caw--one that was repeated every minute or so for good measure--Hazzel would eventually get eyes on the bird again where it had found a shelf of red rock to perch on, along the flank of one of the buttes jutting up over the otherwise flat land. By the time he reached where the crow waited, he'd be able to hear the muffled, faint sounds the wind carried around the large formation that betrayed men making camp. The occasional whiff of something being cooked on the fire whose smoke trail could just barely be glimpsed where it slithered up into the sky above the top of the stone. The base of the butte was too wide to really be able to simply peak around without risking being seen--and it would appear the moondevil had not tamed this particular throat into having much success with language. At least not as fluently as they could navigate words on the tongue of a coyote. But they didn't drop the form just yet, in case it could serve further use; the changes were too drastic to not be taxing in a finite sort of way that had to be managed less liberally. Still, the crow had the means to communicate, in their own way. At Hazzel's arrival, they turned to peck at the rock until a stone crumbled off it. Fluttering after it, they snagged it in their beak and hopped to a relatively clear spot in the dirt, placing the red sandstone with a deliberate sort of intentionality. A sharp flutter of wings carried them off elsewhere momentarily, and they returned with one leaf from the nearby bushes at a time, placing those with purpose as well before flying off to collect another. They completed this with efficient timing, leaving the arrangement as a circle of four leaves placed to the side of the chunk of sandstone. Another handful of fluttering errands brought smaller stones of dusty quartz gravel one at a time, placed inside the ring of leaves; seven in total. Then a last errand, this one taking marginally longer, as it involved harvesting twigs from the bushes and snapping them to a specific length, bringing those pieces back to place inside that circle of leaves as well, all concentrated to the area nearest the sandstone chunk, unlike the quartz pebbles. Four total. They stepped back with this concluded, head tilting to look it over a moment, before finally falling still again, beady black gaze turning up to Hazzel to see how well he spoke crow.
Woozy 1/5/2026 1:14 PM
Hazzel shrugged and tipped back the rest of the water, draining it dry. He’d need more before they left, surely, but if they were successful in their thorough meddling with the caravan here, he imagined they’d be able to spare just enough time to swipe some of their water for himself. Worst came to worst, they could always find a dirty, pitiful puddle to fill it from. Perks of having a magic waterskin. He swung himself off of his mount to stand while they panted and regulated their breathing, taking a few slow steps to settle his body towards walking again. Sue made herself familiar with the local scrub brushes, delicate lips pulling the sparse foliage in for a nibble even as she began to disappear and fade away. Hazzel let her fade; she earned a break just as much as they did. He lingered there while they flew off, shading his eyes briefly against the sky before settling in. Though he took out his pipe to chew on the end, he didn’t light it. Not now. He wasn’t going to let a smoke trail be what gave away the gambit. There’d be plenty of time to smoke later. Thankfully it didn’t take too long for that crow song to reach him, and he tucked the pipe away as he hustled over to where it sat. The closer he got the slower he went until he picked carefully with a level of silence that was self-aware. The stomach grumbles elicited by those stray whiffs of food went ignored, too, orange-brown eyes sharp and alert. He settled up against the butte, watching the crow work patiently. The pipe was fished out again to nestle back between his teeth, chewing as he thought. “Seven folk, four wagons and four horses, is that right?” He whispered, careful not to let his voice carry to the caravan. “Few different ways we could do this.. I could go on in, distract em in the center. Either wow em with tricks, or hollering fear and fury. Could also call em out one at a time, pick em off from the outside.” He mused out load, watching the crow for its reaction. It surely had a few ideas of its own as well.
Daarka 1/5/2026 1:56 PM
The crow seemed pleased with how fluent Hazzel was in bird charades. This was not a stab in the dark idea; this was showing Hazzel the ropes.

Daarka 1/5/2026 10:46 PM
The crow watched him a moment, thoughtful—before abruptly taking off again. This time, it flew out of sight around the butte, and left Hazzel alone for another five minutes or so. Maybe a bit more. Right before Hazzel started to worry he was meant to follow, there was a soft rustle behind him, and what was rapidly no longer resembling anything bird-like steadied to its feet again, rising to a larger height in a slough of feathers and hair. Simple ropes, nothin’ a knife can’t quickly go through,” they rasped, as the dry crunch of their boots carried them over to where the crow had previously set up their little demonstration. “I have done it once or twice,” they added wryly. “And it’s easier than grabbin’ the horses. They just gotta be cut loose; they spook easy after that. They’ll run right through them wagons like they ain’t solid wood if somethin’ scary enough is behind ‘em. Take care of themselves—just gotta be loose.” They dropped to a squat over the dirt. “Men take a bit more convincin’. Some of ‘em go towards the sound of a woman. They goad each other into the bravery to see what it was. When the first one don’t come back, someone else gets sent after ‘em. When he don’t come back neither, you got everyone else’s attention on the same place—always the direction away from the horses. Lets you have more options for how them tethers cut loose. Now,” they added, “You make ‘em think bandits out there, or somethin’ threatenin’ of the sort, they’ll fire into the darkness. So it’s gotta be somethin’ they walk towards with guns holstered.” Yellow eyes—piercing in the near-gone light of the dwindling sunset—lifted back to Hazzel’s. “Ain’t a role here I haven’t played. Untyin’ the horses sounds simpler, but depends on just how unnoticeable you capable of bein’; if a head turns towards you at the wrong moment, or a horse decides it ain’t like you and makes a fuss, you’re made. On the other hand… lurin’ could take many forms, but you gotta be able to put ‘em down fast and quiet when they reach you, or else they’ll spook like the horses. Ain’t gonna lure more after that.” Their head tipped contemplatively. “Got aces up my sleeve to make either a guarantee. Just not both. So you tell me: which one you want to guarantee works, and which you confident you could make work?” (edited)
Woozy 1/6/2026 10:55 PM

Woozy 1/7/2026 3:18 PM
With no input other than the blessing to use free will, shadows shifted until Hazzel took the form of a pale young woman with mousey brown hair pulled back into a bun, with a few loosened strands loosened by a long day. Daarka 1/7/2026 11:50 PM
They watched the old man become a young woman with genuine appreciation for how seamlessly he fell into the guise—not unlike the beat of approval that had washed over the demon to find Hazzel’s method of long distance travel could keep up with their own. Beggars couldn’t be choosers when you needed even just a single ally to be able to survive—but it felt good that the one they’d stumbled into didn’t weigh them down, but kept stride with them more or less effortlessly. Short of fully resurrecting their family, it was about the best arrangement they could have asked for. And with how their luck had been going, Hazzel was about to have his head blown off in an ensuing shootout here. Putting a pin in that grim pessimism, the moondevil turned as Hazzelina picked her way off through the desert, shape sloughing back to that of a coyote, whom no man would think twice about glimpsing here. They picked their way around to the opposite side of the caravan as where the uncanny woman had set herself up, one ear swiveled towards the wagons and listening to the murmuring voices on the other side—which paused at the sound of a woman’s voice, so innocuous yet so illogical out here, with the nearest civilization well over a day’s travel in any direction. Those voices within the wagon circle were hushed when they resumed. Uncertain. By the time the coyote had reached the other side of the wagon the horses were tied to, they had drawn in closer, their paws soft over the baked clay ground—though the men inside the campsite were too preoccupied with the strange woman’s call to even be listening in this direction. After an uncomfortable delay, the coyote listened as a man called back, moving in the direction of the voice, towards a break between the wagons like a gate to the open, darkened desert. “… Ma’am? You lost out here…?” Coyote unfolded back to a human, though that form lasted only a moment before it disappeared entirely. Moving even more carefully without padded feet to muffle their steps, they maneuvered deftly around the end of the wagon until they stood, invisible, adjacent to the nearest horse. It sagged on its feet, half asleep—though the one tied next to it seemed to have begun to catch the moondevil’s scent. Not enough to trigger its prey instincts. But a gentle flare of nostrils, a curious pivot of ears that didn’t quite know what they were sensing yet. Most other fiends would have spooked the animals by now, by virtue of simply what they were, and a beast’s affinity for sensing that evil. But long before coyotes had been given the words of man to lie to them with, they had known how to lie to animals. Their scent itself, the nuanced pheromones men couldn’t detect, even the indescribable sixth-sense presence of their basest nature—all were in on the ruse. All had learned to change their shape just as their flesh had. So they held still a moment, watching the horse’s nostrils wonder at that charlatan of a scent. But the muscles of its neck stayed loose, its ears stayed relaxed. It was curious—but had no reason to fear. It smelled the same harmless dog that children and their pets saw. (edited)

Daarka 1/8/2026 2:25 PM
Listening to those semi-distant calls had the moondevil distractedly appreciating their own lack of a name. If someone yelled one at them over and over again like the treatment Paul was getting, they’d never want to hear it again the rest of their long life—let alone identify by it. But then, life was so much easier when you knew someone by their scent. They didn’t need to introduce themselves, tell you what to call them by. And you really didn’t need to have something to call them by nearly as much as men presumed. They had been at the caravan-hunting life so long that they could afford to muse at the more philosophical matters as their invisible hands dipped into the wagon the horses were tied to, where someone had left some personal affects slumped against the more formally packaged supplies. This step—this step wasn’t normally part of the process. But the normal process also involved a communal knife or two that hadn’t made it back to the survivor’s person before the others had been skinned and looted. There was never a need for everyone to have their own things, because there was never a time when the others weren’t there to pass it around. But the survivor had no qualms with poaching the deficit. The leather bag was quietly flipped open, and a hand snaked in exploratively. It didn’t take long before they found what they had gambled was in the supplies of a traveler: a hunting knife. Pulling it out—and then slowing to use both hands so the strap it was sheathed on didn’t loudly slither across the wagon—they gathered it in their arms, where the tangle of leather and knife hilt bled into the invisibility cloaking their form. Once it was in hand, it stayed unseen as they unsheathed it, carefully shifting forward to grasp the lead of the nearest horse so it held steady and didn’t jostle with their meddling, before beginning the almost gentle process of sawing the knife through the rope just in front of where it was knotted. It was nothing their fingers couldn’t have untied, but they had the experience to know that some horses didn’t take too kindly to the movement of their ropes magically untying themselves under invisible hands that smelled like a stranger. By the time they had severed each of the four ropes, carefully weaving their body around the resting animals like a snake, they noted the faintest hint of blood on the wind. Getting back out again was as simple as invisibly melting back to a coyote and slipping out under the wagon, the horses’ leads dangling inconspicuously limp where even the animals themselves hadn’t noticed. A quick, near-silent trot skirted them around the camp again and back towards the side Hazzelina was doing her little show on—and they very quickly took notice of the body draining into the sand. Spotting the shape of the charlatan still doing her act, they lifted their muzzle to give a rasping whisper that the wind picked up, carrying cleanly to the ear of their companion: “This the part with artistic liberties.”
Woozy 1/19/2026 2:19 PM

Daarka 1/19/2026 11:04 PM
Hazzelina might not have had eyes on the coyote, but it had eyes on her. It watched with rapt curiosity as she crafted her mischief, an involuntarily little sway of its tail as an earnestly impressed beat of approval made the precipice of action they stood on all the more exciting. In their long history of comparable schemes, their pack had dabbled in all sorts of lures and illusions--but the demon hadn't really realized that they did not typically feign being prey. At least, that realization came in the spike of adrenaline at the scream the freshly-tattered woman gave off, like it was the squeal of a mouse. It spurred them into action immediately with the efficiency of a deep-seated instinct--though, fortunately for their partner, they were not enough of a slave to that prey drive to go after the source of the enticing sound. Understandably, that scream immediately sent the camp on their guard, alarm shocking through their numbers as a horrified hush fell over them, leaving only the sounds Hazzelina and her minion made. The moondevil's paws were too deft on the sand they were made for to draw attention over those noises as the coyote sped through the darkness, a dry breeze kicking up in their wake until it practically ran alongside them. Wind that raked its talons through that fire, clawing it out of its bed, pulling it towards its original owner and stretching its glowing tendrils through the air until they were too thin to breathe and the shadows consumed them. The coyote's dash continued across the campsite and under an opposite wagon again, the theft of the fire suddenly cloaking the camp in a darkness it hadn't been equipped for. The jolt of action had roused the horses, but they had not yet been convinced the camp was unsafe. Without breaking stride, the moondevil wove back around towards them, no barrier of light to carefully skirt any longer. When they leapt towards the nearest horse, they had dropped the entirety of the ruse the animal had been lulled into complacency by moments prior; not only had their body stretched and grown monstrous enough that even the humans would know better, but every subtler mask they wore towards animals had gone bare. They struck too fast for the horse to avoid the enormous teeth that clamped in and jerked violently at their throat--but that horse knew precisely what had hit it. The other three spooked like a shockwave, and the demon dropped the first's neck after its point was made; it would likely survive the injury, but it would do so somewhere else. The moondevil didn't give chase as the four horses took off like a shot in a blind, scrambling panic of mortal terror, hot blood spattered to the sand beneath where the monster pivoted, unwilling to stand still long enough for the men to gather enough of their senses to start firing in that direction. In that split-second pivot of continued momentum, they turned their attention entirely on the remaining travelers (and Hazzel), little more, to a human's eye, than a vague and unsettlingly large black shape against the barely-perceptible wagons in a sea of shadow.

Daarka 1/20/2026 10:14 AM

Woozy 1/20/2026 10:29 AM

Shifting their position as their figure buckled and wilted into that of a more ordinary person, they adjusted so they were seated upright in the dirt, twisting to look at the bullet lodged in the side of their upper thigh. It had only made it an inch or so in, like that sloughing vessel had caught it the way a sticky trap would instead of being torn open further by it. The taupe denim sat snugly around the wound, stained in blood that had always been red, actually. Their thick claws had little trouble prying the bullet out again, releasing a fresh but small flow of blood before that quieted once more. “Gotta hike back now you ran us out here—” they didn’t sound too torn up about it, “—might as well see if they got anything worth filchin’ on ‘em, but I wanna find what silver and such they were bringin’ and do away with that. Take what can be fenced without drawin’ attention, probably bury the rest. When we’re through, I’ll bring in a cleanup crew to muddy the scene and keep it from goin’ to waste. Hard part now,” they went on, attention shifted to the bullet hole up on the side of their ribs, “is gatherin’ the steam for the run back. Should get there by morning. Be able to show our faces again in town. Then sleep,” they rumbled with a deep appreciation for that promise. The bullet in their ribs had not gotten in much farther than the one in their leg, but necessitated dragging the ruddy poncho off over their head for once. The button down shirt beneath it was one Hazzel had not yet seen in its entirety, as that poncho had been a consistent feature of this form. It might have been white once, but the desert dust seemed to linger in the fibers so inseparably that it was varying shades of ivory and tan. The end of the sleeves were not a new sight to Hazzel, of course, bearing a muted pattern in dusky blue and teal with warmer orange and red accents—but that same pattern was echoed around the shoulders and collar of the shirt, too, a comparatively rarer sight. Dingy sand-colored buttons were done up all the way, though the buttons ended low enough down their neck to let the collar spread out relaxed. Poncho pooled into their lap for the moment, they twisted to get a better look at the wound in their side, contorting their pose only ever so slightly uncannily to twist their claws within reach of that bullet, too. It was more apparent there than it had been on the denim over their thigh—but here, the linens looked like they hung airy and loose over their form before being tucked into the waist of their pants. Yet despite that looseness, it was like the mouth of the bullet hole was melded to the fabric it had punctured. Normally such an endeavor would have been easiest with the shirt removed entirely—but there was no need for that when the shirt was not in the way, the bloodstains muddying where fabric ended and raw, oozing meat began. The crude gesture sent some of the silver bars scattering loose from the confines of the crate, each just about an inch thick and three long. Each bar amounting to maybe half a dozen gold pieces in value, each sized just right for palming into a pocket--but too many for all of them to be swiped that way. And the serial numbers stamped into the metal didn't make fencing a promising venture when this shipment was found conspicuously missing. At least, not as they were. But the demon's body language made it clear how little they were interested in fiddling with the metal personally, leaving it where they had thrown it to go back to raiding the rest of the wagons in an attempt to find any other such crates. "'Cept one that didn't go swimmin', I reckon," they rumbled in pointed curiosity. (edited)
Woozy 1/27/2026 12:57 PM
Fable exhaled a laugh at the coyote’s successful rumble, subtly nodding his head. “I knew if I went in that water I’d die, so I didn’t. I hid as best I could… luck always runs out eventually, though. Even when you hold all the dice. Eventually Yule of Fire found me.” He spat, ears flattening a little. “Tusk clan’s special princess, last heir of the Flame. A zealot. She found me, and said she’d make sure I couldn’t run ever again. Ripped my leg off, ‘n paraded around with it like her prize toy sayin she was going to make me Dive the next day… but joke’s on her. I snuck into the heart of Tusk and stole it back, right out from under her nose.” “The world tree must’ve taken pity on me. Either that, or even that ancient son of a bitch woke up long enough to yawn at just the right time. A pathway out opened up; a den carved right through the wood. No one expected me to slip out through the center of the world in the heart of Tusk territory. From there I experienced true freedom. Somethin about the void… quiets the madness, in a way. Has its own voices, but after a while they start to harmonize, almost. Like a choir warming up. Didn’t stop the hunger though. That’ll stick, I think.” He paused, sipping his coffee, before murmuring. “Still worth it.”
Daarka 1/27/2026 1:34 PM
As Fable's story drew to a conclusion of sorts, the moondevil slowly returned to their meal, still digesting the one Fable had given them. The kind of eating they engaged in didn't often have opportunity for pleasant conversation between bites--because there wasn't much time between bites in the first place. But they also ate fast. Within a few short minutes, not only had the body been largely disappeared, but the wagon inferno behind them had collapsed to little more than sleepily simmering embers and the occasional curl of green flame. No smoke, notably. Finally sitting back and licking over their gore-stained face repeatedly, the moondevil turned to amble over to the more civil fire Hazzel had restarted, taking their seat where the third coffee cup had been set out. In a lazy slough of bloody pelt, they joined the other two at that fire in a form better suited for drinking coffee--even though their more humanoid face was still stained crimson. "Think I mentioned we was an insulated bunch," they finally broke the silence again, leaning back against a discarded saddle. "Coyotes have their families," they went on, gaze shifting to clock the glimpse of eyeshine reflecting in the firelight as a shadowed creature or two passed between the wagons, "We had our family. But wasn't nothin' else out here really quite like us. We was never estranged from the good coyotes in our midst--hell, we ain't even estranged from the hares. From beetles. We could relate to them... but they couldn't relate to us. Men couldn't relate to us, either--but them's one we never related to ourselves in the first place." They paused for a sip of the coffee, before giving a small, lazily dismissive gesture. "Point I'm makin' is we ain't designed to make friends. We've got what we were given--you talk 'bout your clans, and I'd hazard to guess I can understand that kind of thing. I see it all around here--we just ain't got any fancy labels for it. No gods playin' favorites." Again, their gaze wandered after the shapes of the summoned coyotes that circled the camp, assessing its value, locating the food, gauging whether those still lingering within were a deal-breaking threat. The moondevil didn't seem fussed about it, confident they'd deduce what they needed to. "Just born with the ones we got." A beat, before they drew their gaze back to land on Fable, on the little glints of orange that couldn't be mistaken in his more elusive form. "So I speak with a whole lot'a inexperience in such matters. As someone who ain't never had a need or want to get to know a soul I ain't know since birth. I can own that naïveté--but I'll still boldly presume that gettin' to know new folk ain't usually come with such understandin'." They tipped their head a little. Glanced at Hazzel. "... I imagine if he got a taste of the way I feel the endless hunger in this desert, the way life outside the walls of men is in a constant state of starvation, the way that feeling don't even stay livin' in my gut, but permeates the air around me and the soil underfoot in a way I can't believe he can't feel..." then back to Fable, "I reckon he's about as equipped to expect what that's like as I am to understand your hunger. Got enough of the means to imagine, and know I ain't got the capacity to grasp it proper. But I can imagine." Another pause for another, unhurried sip of coffee. A glance aside without moving their head to watch a coyote slither around a wagon on the tips of its toes, sniffing at its wheels. Then that gleaming yellow flicked back to Fable with a fresh thought. "... You know, this whole time, I been assumin' he was the one who let me in," they went on, a tip of their head towards Hazzel. "That his bleedin' heart spearheaded that arrangement 'cause I looked like a sad enough sack of shit. Now I ain't so sure," they rasped with a toothy grin behind their mug. (edited)

Woozy 1/28/2026 8:43 AM
"You did look like a sad sack of shit," Hazzel murmured a good-natured tease, earning a laugh from the shade. He'd noticed the other eyes starting to make themselves known on the outskirts of the shadows. On his own they'd have warranted a bit more of a conscious eye on them, just in case they got any bright ideas about going after an adult man, but neither demon seemed particularly worried and that was enough to lower his guard down to a casual observer. There were perks to hanging out with powerful fiends. Echo might have been built for the desert, built to trick animals with their scent and nature, Fable used no such methods to hide his infernal nature. But he stayed still in the center, only rarely moving to take another sip of coffee. At least that answered Hazzel's question on who the rest of the cleanup crew would be tonight. With that distraction squarely taken care of, he listened in on their words. It took more attention than he anticipated, with how long the demon seemed to take on getting to the point. Far be it from him to complain about it, though. There was meaning within those words, and the coyote only seemed to have one setting to get around to them with. And what he heard earned a slow smile, the more the meaning behind the words became clear. It was true, he didn't have a concept of an all body, singular hunger that gripped both him and the desert. He was man enough to foolishly believe he could imagine it, and smart enough otherwise to know that it'd come nowhere close to the actual experience. One of Fable's large ears had perked the other demon's way as they spoke up, their gaze taking a few moments longer to pull free from the sky. Similarly he had no complaints as to how they spoke their piece, and patiently sat through it until their points were made. Until that teasing jab rasped his way, and Hazzel replied in turn. Fable barked out a laugh, toothily grinning back. "He certainly does have the bleeding heart of the two of us. It was a joint decision though.. and I'd say we all lucked out in the end there."
Daarka 1/28/2026 11:43 AM
They rattled a thoughtful hum of a sound, taking another drink as they turned to watch two coyotes sneak forward to give experimental tugs at one of the corpses’ hands, their eyes locked on the group around the campfire in caution. But when nothing rose to drive them away, they grew bolder, starting to tear into that flesh and make pieces disappear with an efficient urgency that would now feel familiar for Hazzel and Fable. “Wonder how angels taste,” the moondevil mused suddenly, glancing back Fable’s way. “How they squeal.” They were under no illusion a lesser celestial was some substitute for a god, but Fable had certainly intrigued their appetite. “I’d wager they got—what them fuckers call it… white meat. The winged things always do.”
Woozy 2/2/2026 12:28 AM
Fable let out a similarly inquisitive sound at the moondevil's stray thought, an ear picking up briefly towards the sounds of the coyotes eating their fill. "Never had an angel before." He laughed at the speculation, finishing his coffee. "White meat... I imagine it'll have something of the same to its flavor. Still marinated in divine energy, even if it ain't the source. Probably tastes real rich. Succulent." "Buttery," Hazzel helpfully added, polishing off the rest of some scavenged cheese. Fable out a pin in his speculation lest he start drooling, and cleared his throat instead. "... Might need to keep an ear out for one making itself present. Just in case." "I'll keep an ear out then. Haven't heard of any angels round these parts lately though. What about you?" Hazzel said as he glanced to the moondevil.
Daarka 2/2/2026 1:07 AM
They chuckled dryly. “The hell you take me for? You think these elbows are rubbin’ those of the divine? If some heavenly choir could be fucked to come down here and punish their lil’ sinners—shit, I’d ask how I could be of assistance. Mind my manners real good. But then, I ain’t never had an angel put a bullet in me,” they added wryly. “Divinity left this land to govern itself. Them chapel-dwellers make an art outta wastin’ their breath.” A beat. “But if I catch the trail of somethin’ higher and mightier, you’ll be the first to know,” they promised in a purring lilt. “Long as you swear you’ll save a bite or two for me. Until then, I’ll just have to keep makin’ these mere sinners fill the void.”
Woozy 2/2/2026 5:13 PM
It was, admittedly, about the answer Hazzel expected. Even down to the offer to help punish all those foul wayward worshippers, with hands making a righteous mess of the desert. Then again, with how long the moondevils had been here, maybe those same angels had looked down and saw justice was mete out enough for their tastes, their quotas. Why come down from the heavens to toil in the dirt when you could outsource punishment to something already so willing to take up the mantle? But then, he had a feeling he already knew the truth; they didn't care enough about what happened here to lift a finger. Neither to help nor hinder.
